Filed in maths/books/indonesia.md (FULLY READ + CITATION CHECK PASSED). Teacher evidence only: the matched companion guide of the Grade 10 Revisi student book cited in this lesson — Panduan Umum (printed pp. 1–28) plus one guide section per Revisi chapter (Bab 1 pp. 29–62; Bab 2 pp. 63–102; Bab 3 pp. 103–124; Bab 4 pp. 125–148; Bab 5 pp. 149–198; Bab 6 pp. 199–242; Bab 7 pp. 243–286) with objectives, achievement criteria, suggested lesson hours, prerequisite notes, teaching guidance and full answer keys. It teaches no pupil content beyond the student book. Negative evidence: the guide has no vectors chapter, and it handles the student book's Bab 1 logarithms enrichment only by an external link — it is not cited for vectors or logarithms teaching. Guide citation stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.

| The arithmetic mean defined (worked: (10 + 8 + 15 + 16 + 6)/5 = 55/5 = 11); the mean of frequency-table data, x̄ = Σnᵢxᵢ / Σnᵢ, and the approximate mean of classed data computed with the class centres in place of the values; a complete worked survey example combining everything — 30 students' marks: frequency table and exact mean, bar chart, a five-class table with width 4, the approximate mean from class centres, a histogram, the ascending cumulative table and its bar representation; overlapping-means problems in the exercises (from the mean of the first four tests, the mean of the last three and the mean of all six, find the fourth test) | Syria, Grade 8 Algebra student's book, printed pp. 111–117, 123 |
| The median of a sample (وسيط, M) defined: for an odd count 2n + 1 it is the (n + 1)-th ordered value; for an even count 2n it is the mean of the n-th and (n + 1)-th ordered values (worked: M = 12 for an odd sample; M = (13 + 14)/2 = 13.5 for an even one); the median from a frequency table using a cumulative-frequency table (26 pupils' maths marks give M = 41, the 13th and 14th values both 41); the range of a sample (مدى, E): the difference between its largest and smallest values (E = 49 − 37 = 12); quartiles defined: Q2 is the median, Q1 the median of the lower sub-sample and Q3 the median of the upper, so the three quartiles split the ordered sample into four equal-count parts (worked: Q1 = 6.5, Q2 = 9, Q3 = 11.5); about 50% of a sample's values lie between Q1 and Q3; the arithmetic mean used as prior knowledge in the closing exercises (a mark list with mean 10.3, median 9.5 and range 9) | Syria, Grade 9 Algebra student's book, printed pp. 108–116 |

**Acceptance count: taught in 74 countries (Egypt, Jordan, Greece, Libya, Vietnam, Sierra Leone, Mauritania, Laos, Yemen, Indonesia, Nicaragua, Ethiopia, South Sudan, Iraq, Bangladesh, Nepal, Cambodia, Kuwait, Iran, China, Pakistan, Syria, Malaysia, Rwanda, Sudan, India, Mongolia, Venezuela, North Macedonia, Afghanistan, Uzbekistan, Ecuador, Albania, Bolivia, Tajikistan, Palestine, Turkey, Tanzania, Armenia, South Africa, Kazakhstan, El Salvador, Cyprus, Sri Lanka, Colombia, Angola, Moldova, Australia, Brazil, Bhutan, Cabo Verde, Costa Rica, Cuba, Bulgaria, Eritrea, Chile, Finland, Dominica, Dominican Republic, Guyana, Gabon, Ireland, Fiji, Honduras, Italy, Iceland, Mozambique, Peru, Suriname, Papua New Guinea, Thailand, Uganda, Samoa, United States).** Egypt carries the mean as a balance point, median/mode/outliers, the range, and quartiles with the semi-interquartile range, the centre of data in Prep 1's new curriculum, central tendency in Prep 3's old series, and the least-squares method in Prep 3's new curriculum (the Prep rows were completed by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep); Jordan carries quartiles with the box-and-whisker plot and dispersion for grouped data; Greece carries the full ladder from mean to coefficient of variation in three books; Libya carries the weighted mean and the four dispersion measures; Vietnam carries central tendency and spread for ungrouped and grouped samples; Sierra Leone carries the whole ladder from the JSS mean to the SSS standard deviation and mean deviation; Mauritania carries mean, mean deviation, variance and standard deviation. Indonesia carries averages and spread from mean, median, mode, range, quartiles and IQR in the Grade 8 Merdeka student book, through grouped mean, median, mode, quartiles, percentiles, variance and standard deviation in Grade 10, to regression and correlation in Grade 11. Its Grade 7 Merdeka book teaches NO mean/median/mode, so Indonesia enters this lesson from Grade 8 and Grade 10. (Wording corrected by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep: the Grade 8 entry is the Merdeka student book itself, Bab 6, printed pp. 255–288, cited directly since the 2026-11-06 gap batch; this sentence previously read "Grade 8 (guide)", from when only the guide was in hand.) Nicaragua carries this lesson only in part — the average (promedio) in Grade 5, with the total found from the average and the average as a decimal; it teaches no median, no mode, no range and no variance anywhere in the set, and its Grade 9 statistics unit is presentation only. Iraq carries averages and spread whole, from mean, median, mode and range in Grade 5 — with the corrected end-problem values (dot-plot frequencies 3, 7, 5, 5, 6; mean 82/26 ≈ 3.15, mode 2, median 3, range 4) — through measures of central tendency and range in Grade 8 on the corrected 15-mark list including the 75, and the best measure of central tendency in Grade 9, to the full statistics chapters in both Grade 10 tracks (mean, median, mode, standard deviation — SD of 1,3,5,7,9 = √8 = 2√2 — with the Scientific track adding the correlation coefficient and the standard score), and standard deviation, Pearson and Spearman correlation and regression in Grade 11 Literary — Spearman and regression being Literary-only, with the regression graph points taught corrected ((4, 8.85) and (8, 5.05)). Nepal carries averages and spread only in part — mean, median and mode with range in Grade 8, mean, median, mode and quartiles in Grade 9 compulsory, and grouped mean, median and quartiles in Grade 10 compulsory, with dispersion — quartile deviation, mean deviation, standard deviation and the coefficient of variation, with box plots — only in the Optional books (discrete series in Grade 9 Optional, continuous series in Grade 10 Optional); Grades 5 and 6 teach no average and no mean/median/mode, and the Grade 7 mean work is taught corrected — the grouped table on p. 172 double-counts 17, 128/10 on p. 175 is 12.8 not 128, and the Ex. 21.4 Q2(ङ) key mean of 93.33 is 126.67. Cambodia carries averages and spread only in part — the mean in Grade 5, mean, mode and median with grouped data in Grades 8–9, quartiles, deciles, percentiles, range, variance and standard deviation with the normal distribution in Grades 11–12, and expectation, variance and standard deviation of discrete probability distributions in the Grade 12 Probability booklet; box plots and the five-number summary, mean deviation, the coefficient of variation and choosing the average are not taught anywhere in the set. Iran teaches the mean alone in Grade 5, grouped means and the range in Grade 8, and the full set — median, mode, variance, standard deviation and quartiles — in the Grade 10 Humanities book, with the box plot and the coefficient of variation in Grade 11 Statistics and Probability. North Macedonia enters from its main set — Grade 5 (2022), Grade 6 (2023) and Grade 7 (2025).

Ghana also contributes to this lesson as curriculum evidence only — Ghana's set holds no pupil textbook — and Ghana is not counted in the taught-in figure above. (Ghana rows stocked by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep.)

Mongolia enters through its ministry textbooks Математик V, VI, VII, VIII, IX, X, XI (Grades 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11): averages from the first mean to the spread measures — range, quartiles and standard deviation — of the senior statistics chapters.

**Flagged wording, not taught:** Sierra Leone's JSS 3 Term 3 Lesson 124 phrases the frequency-table mean as "add the frequencies given and divide by the total number of rows", with no worked frequency-table example to clarify it. This lesson does not teach that wording: it teaches the correct rule from the same book's Lesson 128 — the estimated mean x̄ = Σfx / Σf, value times frequency, divided by the total frequency. Laos carries averages and spread whole in Grade 9 — mean, mode and median with grouped formulas, and range, variance and standard deviation. Yemen carries averages and spread from the arithmetic mean in Grades 6–7, through mean, mode, cumulative frequency and median in Grade 9 and central tendency and dispersion in Grade 10, to correlation and regression in Grades 11–12. Ethiopia carries averages and spread from the mean only in Grade 5, through mean, mode and median in Grade 6 and mean, mode, median and range in Grade 7, to central tendency with grouped means and population variance and standard deviation in Grade 9, central tendency only — median of grouped data, with deciles and percentiles in the guide — in Grade 11, and mean deviation, quartile deviation, variance, standard deviation and the coefficient of variation in Grade 12; the Grade 12 book uses population variance (÷n) throughout, and there are no quartiles, no box plots and no correlation anywhere in Ethiopia. South Sudan carries averages and spread from mean, median and mode in Primary 7, through mean, mode and median with frequency tables and grouped data in Secondary 1, to the assumed mean and quartiles and percentiles read from the ogive in Secondary 2, with the interquartile range from the Secondary 2 teacher's guide — with one correction: Primary 7 p. 69 prints the median of 3, 9, 15, 17, 44 as "150"; the correct median is 15. No range is taught as a named topic in Primary 7; spread is carried by the Secondary 2 quartiles and interquartile range. Bangladesh carries averages and spread only in part — from the average as total ÷ number of items in Class 5, including the weighted-average idea: averaging group averages fails when the group sizes differ (Ch. 8, printed p. 92) — through mean, median and mode in Class 6 and range with class intervals and frequency distribution in Class 7, to mean (direct and assumed-mean methods), median and mode with frequency polygons and cumulative frequency in Class 8 and the full dispersion ladder — range, mean deviation, variance and standard deviation — in Class 9; it teaches no quartiles, no box plot, no interquartile range, no coefficient of variation, no percentiles and no outliers, so those rest on the other countries (corrected by the 2026-11-08 re-verification sweep: an earlier version of this text wrongly listed "no weighted mean" among Bangladesh's absences — Class 5 Ch. 8 teaches the weighted-average idea, printed p. 92) — with corrections taught, never as printed: p. 223 Pearson "1983" (correct 1893); the English-only p. 225 claim that Thomas's family has the much larger age dispersion (Aleya's is larger — variance 1750/6 ≈ 291.67 against Thomas's 2480/31 = 80; the Bangla is correct); the p. 233–234 rickshaw-fares exercise, defective as stated (one value each has no meaningful standard deviation); and the p. 219 "mean deviation determined from the range (Mo)" (should be mode (Mo)), the p. 223 claim that standard deviation uses absolute values (it uses squared deviations), and the p. 232 standard-deviation line with the square-root sign missing (√1.7748 ≈ 1.33, result ≈ 5.33, the book says 5.32). Mean, median and mode are used but never defined in the Class 9 book — their definitions rest on Classes 6 and 8 — and quartile deviation is named only in the Chapter 9 opener diagram (p. 211) and never taught.

**Flagged, not taught as printed:** the Grade 11 teacher guide's Solutions to Exercise 7.14 print Decile D3 as 21.5 (correct 20.15 — the book dropped the division by f_c = 10), First quartile Q1 as 33 (correct 34), and a Decile D2 working that as printed gives 5, not 5.67 (the final value 5.67 is correct for the right inputs); its Exercise 7.15 table prints the cumulative frequency for class 81–90 as 9 (correct 92). The lesson teaches the corrected values. Kuwait carries averages from exploring mean, median, mode and range in Grade 4 to mean, median and mode in Grade 8, including the mean of grouped data from class centres. Band C adds the full descriptive thread in Grade 10 Term 2 (mean, median and mode, quartiles, box plots, variance and standard deviation), the literary-track Statistics thread in Grade 11 Term 2 (quartiles, skewness, dispersion, the empirical rule and z-scores, with its workbook's exercises), workbook exercises on standard deviation and z-scores in Grade 11 Term 1, and — as inference only, not basic averages — estimation, hypothesis testing, correlation and regression in Grade 12 Term 1 (scientific track).

China carries averages and spread in Grade 4 (the mean, by levelling and by total ÷ count) and whole in the senior A-edition Volume 2 — sample estimation, percentiles, mean, median and mode, variance and standard deviation.

Pakistan (Punjab) carries averages and spread from the Class 5 mean to the Class 11 statistics book's full location-and-dispersion treatment — which defines sample variance with denominator n, filed as printed — and on to expectation and variance of random variables and the measures of the normal distribution.

Syria defines the arithmetic mean in Grade 6, extends it to frequency tables and class centres in Grade 8 Algebra — with one complete survey worked through every representation — and adds the median, range and quartiles in Grade 9 Algebra.

Malaysia teaches averages in Form 2 — mode, median and mean, what extreme values do to them, and grouped data with midpoints — then spread in Form 4 for ungrouped data and in Form 5 for grouped data, judging performers by centre and spread together.

Rwanda teaches averages and spread in Senior 1 — mean, mode and median, quartiles and the interquartile range, with estimates from a cumulative frequency diagram — while Primary 4 asks an average in its assessment without teaching a method, and range appears only as a definition in the Primary 4 teacher's guide glossary.

Sudan takes the arithmetic mean, median, mode and modal class in Grade 9, then gives both Grade 12 tracks a full measures course — mean, median, mode and range in Basic; dispersion in Specialized Book One.

India stocks averages and spread from Mathematics Class 7, 9–11: mean; mean x̄ = Σxᵢ/n; mean of a frequency distribution x̄ = Σfᵢxᵢ/Σfᵢ; mean of grouped data by the direct method, x̄ = Σfᵢxᵢ/Σfᵢ, with class marks as representatives; dispersion.

Venezuela enters through the Colección Bicentenario, the MPPE's one-book-per-grade course: *Contando con los recursos* (Grade 4), *La Patria Buena* (Grade 5), *Hecho en Venezuela* (Grade 6), *Matemática Para La Vida* (Grade 7), *Conciencia Matemática* (Grade 8), *La Matemática de la Belleza* (Grade 9), *Naturaleza Matemática* (Grade 10) and *La Matemática y el Vivir Bien* (Grade 11). From its books: from Grade 4: the mean (average) — la media aritmética (promedio) defined and computed over 3 days of tally-mark water use; from Grade 8: definition — mode: the most frequent value/group (10.000–14.999 ml group, total 12, in the example tables); from Grade 11: univariate description recap on two variables separately: mode, median, mean and standard deviation for age and for connection hours.

Afghanistan enters through its Ministry of Education course — the Grade 1 course in two language editions (Pashto and Dari, one work with identical page maps) and Dari student books for Grades 4, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11 and 12, all printing year 1398 HS except the Grade 8 book, which prints 1399 HS on its cover and title page and 1398 HS on its specifications page. From its books: from Grade 7: mode; mean (average), including finding a mark needed to reach a target mean; chapter summary and exercises (statistics); from Grade 8: mean of discrete (ungrouped) data, including the mean from a frequency table; mean of grouped (continuous) data using a frequency table (class midpoints); chapter exercises (statistics); from Grade 10: comparing mean, median and mode on the normal curve; variance; standard deviation; mean (average) deviation.

Albania enters through its Mediaprint teacher's books (Libër Mësuesi, in Albanian) — TEACHER EVIDENCE only: no pupil's book is in hand for any grade, so Albania is never cited as a pupil-textbook source; what the teacher's books evidence is what is taught and in what order, through their lesson plans, worked answers and answer keys. From its books: from Grade 5 (teacher's book): The mode (moda) of a data set. from Grade 6 (teacher's book): : mean (mesatarja), median (mesorja), mode (moda) and range (shtrirja/amplituda) defined and found from data. from Grade 9 (teacher's book): Reading frequency tables; mode, median, mean and range (amplituda) from tables; Comparing data sets by mode, median, mean and range. from Grade 10 (teacher's book): Mean, median, mode, modal class and range; estimating the mean from grouped tables KEY error in the ditare's…. from Grade 12 (teacher's book): Treguesit e pozicionit të qëndrës (Measures of central location: mean, median and mode); Treguesit e shpërhapjes (Measures of spread).

Bolivia enters through the Área Matemática sections of its Ministerio de Educación (Estado Plurinacional de Bolivia) Textos de Aprendizaje — the student books for Grades 7, 8, 10 and 12, each the maths section of that year's integrated year book, keeping the whole book's printed foliation, with no imprint or ISBN printed in any of them — and through the Compendio para Maestras y Maestros 2023–2024, the ministry's teacher compilation of the official student texts for all six Secundaria years (Grades 7–12), cited as teacher-compilation evidence only, never as or in place of a pupil text; its Secundaria 3 and Secundaria 5 sections are the only in-hand source for Grades 9 and 11, whose student texts are not in the record. From its books: from the Compendium's Secundaria 5 section (Grade 11): Estadística descriptiva.

Tajikistan enters through its Ministry-approved pupil's textbooks in Tajik (Cyrillic), all published in Dushanbe — Математика for Grade 5 (Маориф, 2022) and Grade 6 (5th edition, Маориф, 2021), Алгебра for Grades 7 (4th improved edition, 2023) and 8 (4th edition, 2023), and Геометрия — the continuation of stereometry — for Grade 11 (3rd edition, Лавҳ, 2020). From its books: from Grade 5: Arithmetic mean of several numbers; from Grade 7: Arithmetic mean, range and mode of a data set; Median of an ordered data set, odd and even counts.

Palestine enters through the pupil's books of its Ministry of Education (Curriculum Centre, Ramallah), all in Arabic — Mathematics Grade 1 in two parts (both fourth edition, 2020), Grade 10 in two parts (Part One third edition, 2020; Part Two second edition, 2019 — the held parts are different editions and are cited as such), and the Grade 12 Technological-stream book (التكنولوجي, first edition, 2019 — the book's own name for its stream, never "Information Technology"). No ISBN is printed in any of the five books. From its books: from Grade 12 (Technological stream): mean and standard deviation recalled.

Barbados enters as CURRICULUM EVIDENCE only, through the *Primary Mathematics Syllabus* (Classes 1–4) of its Ministry of Education and Human Resource Development — its Class 1 syllabus requires determining the mode for given data — the mean is named only, alongside the mode in the scope-and-sequence item title, with no Class 1 objective attached; Class 2 requires finding the mode for a set of data; Classes 3 and 4 require averages of given data — mean and mode. A syllabus states what is to be taught, in what order and to what depth; it teaches nothing itself. Barbados publishes no government pupil textbooks — primary mathematics books are commercial titles parents buy, and the syllabi's Suggested Texts appendices name them (*Caribbean Primary Mathematics* Levels 1–6; *Nelson Primary Maths for Caribbean Schools* 1–4; *Steps To Common Entrance Mathematics* 1–3) as named by the syllabus only: none is in hand and none is cited. Barbados is therefore named in this lesson's countries line and its syllabus requirements are cited in the table as curriculum evidence, but Barbados is not counted in the taught-in figure.

Turkey enters through the pupil's textbooks of its Millî Eğitim Bakanlığı (MEB — Ministry of National Education), all in Turkish, first volumes (1. Kitap) only — Grade 3 (ISBN 978-975-11-9897-6, TTKB approval 13/07/2026), Grade 6 (ISBN 978-975-11-8773-4, TTKB approval 30.06.2025) and Grade 7 (ISBN 978-975-11-9899-0, TTKB approval 1.07.2026); no edition, printing or year line is printed in any of the three books, and the grades' second volumes (2. Kitap) are not in hand and are cited nowhere. From its books: from Grade 6: data set (veri seti) defined; arithmetic mean (aritmetik ortalama) as equal sharing of marbles among five friends; definition: sum of values ÷ number of values, computed in two stated steps; mean in use: books read over 7 months (6, 7, 9, 9, 10, 10, 12 → mean 9); mean of a stem-and-leaf data set and whether the mean is itself a data value; median (ortanca) motivated by an outlier: book counts 10, 7, 6, 5, 4, 3, 8, 12, 9, 70, 3 — the middle value represents better than the mean; ordering rule discovered; median defined and computed: middle value for odd counts, mean of the two middle values for even counts; range (açıklık) defined as largest − smallest; median preferred when the range is large / outliers present (stated); mode (tepe değer) defined: most frequent value or category; works for categorical data too; a set may have several modes or none (all values equally frequent); mode practice: simit counts with a single mode (85); sibling counts with two modes (2 and 3, five each — verified); distinct values 4, 2, 3, 1, 5, 6, 7 with no mode; choosing the centre: mean for balanced data, mode for frequently repeated values, median when extreme values are present (stated as the selection rule, tried on three sets); full interpretation exercise: reading-books table (type, count, pages) — mean, median, mode type, range of page totals and what each says; checking a printed summary table against three stem-and-leaf displays (TEKNOFEST applications): mean/median/mode for all three categories recomputed and confirmed exact (20/20/20; 16,25/15/20; 13/12/10); centre need not be a data value (mean of 3, 5, 5, 3 is 4, not in the set); centre as a region when mean = median = mode = 10; from Grade 7: Interpreting data against a reference value: body-mass-index reference band used to judge a weekly weight-loss record; A representative value for a data set need not be in the set, need not be a standard centre, but must be justified from the data; Interpreting a distribution using centre and spread together, never the centre alone.

Tanzania enters through the pupil's book of its Tanzania Institute of Education (TIE) — *Mathematics Standard Five — Pupil's Book*, the English-medium Standard Five book (© TIE 2019, published 2019, reprinted 2021; ISBN 978-9987-09-109-6; Certificate of Approval No. 282, approved by the Ministry of Education, Science and Technology on 5 December 2019, prepared to the 2016 primary Mathematics Syllabus). It is the only Tanzania mathematics book in hand: the other 20 recorded works — among them the Swahili-medium Hisabati Std 5, a separate book from this English one — are waiting-for-file and are cited nowhere. From its book: from Standard Five: the average defined as the sum of the item values divided by the number of items and built physically by equal sharing, then practised on ages, numbers, attendances, weights, heights and measures.

Armenia enters through its Algebra 7; Algebra 8 (Mikayelyan); the Algebra 8 methodological guide (teacher evidence; it companions a different, unheld Gogyan student book, not the held Mikayelyan book); Algebra 9. From its books: shortcomings of statistical parameters; statistical data (mean, mode, median of a data set); median of statistical data; percentile and quantile; variance of a data sample and standard (mean-square) deviation.

## Morocco added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Morocco's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **32 books, all of them teacher guides or teacher editions (Dar Attakafa, Casablanca); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 610 teaching items. No Moroccan pupil maths textbook is in hand: the pupil's books are commercial print, and the library's systematic free shelf is Dar Attakafa's teacher-guide library. Every Morocco item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's lesson, its content and its order — cited as the evidence kind it is; Morocco is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Moroccan pupil books are in hand and read. Set reconciliation, as the placements prove it: the 32 are distinct works (Le Monde des Maths ×6, L'Explicite en Maths ×6, Al Moufid primary ×3, Al Marjie primary ×2, Al Moufid collège ×3, Switch On! guides ×6, Switch On! Every Day booklets ×6; French 27 books, Arabic 5); textbooks.tsv carries only 28 of the 32 — a library-record gap, reported here and not edited (merges never edit the records). The primary guides all teach the one official revised programme (July 2020) — 32 lessons per grade, Grades 1–6, stated independently by up to four series per grade; the collège guides give the BIOF programme distributions for Grades 7–9. Read depth, as the placements declare it: the Arabic guides' legacy font encoding makes pdftotext return mojibake, so their contents were read from rendered pages; Books 6, 7 and 25 place their own year's programme distribution only (their readings of the other collège years are excluded, so nothing is double-counted); Book 31 places the distinct rows of its yearly programme table, a coarser grain than the lesson-by-lesson guides. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct lesson/topic the guide teaches the teacher to deliver, and its named exclusions — method/framework chapters, evaluation/bilan/support apparatus and the Switch On confirmation tests, Switch On 1's graphisme weeks, bibliographies, indexes and recording tables — are stocked nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 1 teacher-evidence item from 1 of the guides (Book 25; Grades 9).

**Book 25:** *Al Moufid, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 3ème année du cycle secondaire collégial* (3AC = Grade 9, BIOF) (teacher guide, French) — Morocco, Dar Attakafa (Casablanca); 5,252,673 bytes, 215 PDF pages. Items are the distinct topics of the 3AC distribution (printed p. 92 in the shared programme chapter read in this guide). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Statistics (Al Moufid 3AC guide) | Morocco — *Al Moufid, Guide de l'enseignant(e), 3AC, Grade 9* (teacher guide) — teacher evidence, printed p. 92 |

Morocco enters this lesson as teacher evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every book in Morocco's in-hand set is a teacher guide or teacher edition, not a pupil textbook, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as a pupil's book (the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent). Morocco's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 32 guides are read and stocked, but no pupil book is in hand.

## Cyprus added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Cyprus's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **75 distinct works: 22 pupil textbooks (primary Grades 1–6, part-books) and 53 teacher guides (Οδηγός Εκπαιδευτικού), all Cyprus Ministry of Education (Υπουργείο Παιδείας), in Greek** — and placed 281 teaching items: 228 from the pupil books and 53 teacher-evidence items (one per guide, worded from the guide's stated success indicators, ΔΕΙΚΤΕΣ ΕΠΙΤΥΧΙΑΣ). SET CORRECTION: the expansion map recorded Cyprus Maths as 22 student + 43 teacher = 65 in hand; the records' union proves 22 student + 53 teacher = **75** (staging books.json carries 64 files = 22 student + 42 teacher, all secured in GitHub; textbooks.tsv adds 11 further teacher guides held as git-blob files that are in no books.json: Grade 1 Units 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, Grade 2 Unit 1, Grade 4 Unit 1) — the correction is recorded here; the library records themselves are not edited (merges never edit the records). In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S22 and the guides as Books T1–T53, in grade order. Cyprus COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states the pupil book's unit, its indicators and its lesson organisation — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). The read's three flagged claims are carried exactly: (1) the map's “65” is corrected above; (2) the Grade 3 Unit 1 teacher guide's indicator Αρ2.1 states quantities “to 10,000” where its pupil book's Unit 1 teaches to 1,000 — the guide's figure is NOT confirmed by the pupil book and is worded as the guide's own claim in that guide's row, never as pupil teaching; (3) three guides carry the books' own unit-number mislabels — the Grade 4 Unit 3 guide's contents header reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 3 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 2”, the Grade 6 Unit 9 guide's running head reads “ΕΝΟΤΗΤΑ 8” — the contents match their cover units, so the rows record the true units with the errors noted in the book entries, taught corrected, never as printed. Production date stamps inside three files (Grade 5 Part 1 “22/06/2026”, Grade 6 Part 1's InDesign stamp 05/03/2026, Grade 6 Part 3 “01/07/2026”) are PDF production stamps in the ministry files as held, not edition dates. The placements' named exclusions — front matter, icon keys, contents pages, cut-out card appendices (“Για τη σελίδα …”), Διαμορφωτική Αξιολόγηση (formative assessment) pages and enrichment sets repeating unit content — are stocked nowhere; the apparent “330C/20C” in the Grade 3 Part 1 enrichment is the degree sign merging with the digits in the text layer (33 °C falling by 2 °C), not a book error. Teacher-guide coverage in hand: Grades 1–3 Units 1–12 complete, Grade 4 Units 1–3 only, Grade 5 Units 1–4, Grade 6 Units 1–10; the further guides that exist only as ministry hub links (Grade 4 Units 4+, Grade 5 Units 5+, Grade 6 Units 11+) are not in hand, not read and not counted. Cyprus's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (75/75) has been read and placed; if the hub-link guides are later secured, Cyprus reverts to PARTIAL until they are read.

## Sri Lanka added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

Pass 1 read the expansion map's 59 files (32 works); Pass 2 pinned 35 further in-hand files the map's subject filter had missed — 34 NIE teacher guides for Grades 1–13 and one pupil textbook (Book S26, filed under the subject "Science for Technology") — and read every one. In the sections below the pupil books are cited as Books S1–S26 and the guides as Books T1–T40. Sri Lanka COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure only on the strength of its pupil-book rows; its guide rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — a guide states its syllabus's competencies, its lesson sequence and its teaching instructions — cited as the evidence kind they are and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Set facts carried exactly: (1) the Grade 9 Sinhala pupil book is held only as chapter files of two different printings (Books S9–S10) whose shared chapter 20 is a different chapter in each, and NO chapter-26 file exists in either split — the English twin's chapter 26 is Algebraic fractions; the hole is carried as a set gap, not as read; (2) the 2018 Sinhala Grade 9 teacher guide is filed twice (two scans of one work) and is placed once (Book T2), and the file held as the Grade 7 2008 manual's "Tamil" copy is byte-identical to the English manual (same git blob SHA) and is cited once, as the English manual (Book T10); (3) the year-less Tamil Grade 4 and Grade 5 guides are proved by byte size to be NIE's 2015 editions, not the far larger 2019/2020 scans NIE also lists (Books T39–T40); (4) the 1976 Grade 12 Mathematics Part I scan (Book S23) ends with some six pages of an unrelated German textbook's verb tables (PDF pp. 271–276) — its mathematics runs to PDF p. 270 and its last chapters were sampled, not contents-verified; (5) the Grade 1 Sinhala 2026 guide is in hand as a Term 1 volume only (Book T33) — its Terms 2–3 are stocked nowhere — and the Grade 1 Tamil 2016 guide (Book T31) is marked DRAFT on its cover and is placed as filed; (6) the Grade 11 guide's Term 2 table labels one lesson (competency 23.6, inside the geometry cluster) "Central limit theorem" — recorded as printed in Book T19's entry; no row in this course rests on that label; (7) Grade 10 Mathematics Part I (English) and Grade 7 Mathematics Part I (Tamil, 2007–2015 edition) are NOT in hand in any filing — library gaps, not unread books; their twins in the other medium are held and read (Books S17 and S13/S14). ## Colombia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Colombia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **27 distinct works: 26 student books and 1 teacher guide, all Colombia Ministerio de Educación Nacional (MEN), in Spanish** — Escuela Nueva Matemáticas cartillas Grades 1–5 (11), Nivelemos Matemáticas student workbooks Grades 1–4 (4), Aulas sin fronteras Grade 6 Unidad 1 student book and its teacher guide (2), Postprimaria student guides Grades 6–9 (4), Secundaria Activa student modules Grades 6–9 (4), and Vamos a Aprender Matemáticas 10 and 11 (2) — 3,928 PDF pages — and placed 293 teaching items: 288 from the student books and 5 teacher-evidence items from the one teacher guide (Book 23). SET CORRECTIONS carried exactly: (1) the expansion map's “28 student + 1 teacher = 29” over-counts by 2 — textbooks.tsv carries duplicate records of Escuela Nueva Grado 1 Cartilla 1 and Grado 2 Cartilla 1 (old blob/main rows for the same two works that also landed as release assets); the distinct set is 27 works and the duplicates are cited once (the library records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records); (2) the set in hand contains NO Grade 3 Cartilla 3 — Grade 3 holds Cartillas 1–2 plus the Nivelemos Grade 3 workbook, and Cartilla 2 completes Unidades 6–9; nothing in hand is a mislabelled Cartilla 3, and no never-published ruling is needed or made; (3) Nivelemos Matemáticas Grado 1 (Book 10) has NO asset in any GitHub release — it was read from the MEN source file itself, landed at exactly the census size (5,020,415 bytes) with EOF intact; the library side still owes that file a GitHub landing. The two Vamos a Aprender books (Books 26–27) are image-only viewer assemblies with no text layer, read from rendered pages (covers, printed contents pp. 6–7 and body spot-checks — Matemáticas 10 printed p. 110 “Teorema del seno”, Matemáticas 11 printed p. 120 “Derivada de una función en un punto”); printed page = PDF page in the sampled range. In the sections below the books are cited as Books 1–27 in the placements' order. Colombia COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of its student-book rows; Book 23's rows are TEACHER EVIDENCE — the guide companions Book 15 with teaching plans, activity scripts and answers and teaches no student content itself — cited as the evidence kind it is and never the basis of the count (the standing Ghana evidence precedent for guide rows). Book errors: none material at placement grain — the Secundaria Activa Grade 9 module's contents page misprints two of its own headings (“Captítulo”, “Undiad 2”); the content is unaffected and rows use the corrected unit names. The placements' named exclusions — presentation letters, “how this book works” pages, author/imprint pages, transversal civics/health/environment themes, practice/evaluation apparatus (“Practica más”, “Evaluación del aprendizaje”, answer keys) and the Escuela Nueva “Estándares/Competencias” boxes at each unidad opening (MEN's own curriculum-standard statements; the taught content is placed at the guías themselves) — are stocked nowhere. The Círculos de Aprendizaje integrated books and the Retos para Gigantes Transición weekly guides are NOT in this set (filed as integrado, not matematicas) and are stocked nowhere. Colombia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (27/27) has been read and placed, on the in-hand definition.

## Andorra added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Andorra's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them official Escola Andorrana curriculum programmes published by decree in the Butlletí Oficial del Principat d'Andorra (BOPA); 0 pupil (student) books** — and placed 679 curriculum-requirement items. A programme states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Andorra item in this section is therefore TEACHER EVIDENCE — a curriculum requirement, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is; Andorra is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until Andorran pupil books are in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded 3 books in hand; the true set is 4 — the grades 1–6 programme (Decret 376/2021, Annex VII) is held in the GitHub repo but sits in no staging books.json, which is why the map missed it. All four programmes are Andorra's OWN (Govern d'Andorra, in Catalan); the Spanish and French systems also taught in Andorra (Spain's Anaya and Innovamat manuals; France's Le Livre Scolaire) are list-only names on official school book lists — no file is held, so they are cited nowhere in this course and credited to no country — and the Escola Andorrana itself has no commercial pupil-textbook series, so programmes are all that exist to hold. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct stated requirement — each specific competency, and each distinct entry in the programmes' learning-resource tables, tagged by cycle where the table splits by cycle — and a procedure entry that merely restates a concept entry is folded into it, not double-counted. The placements' named exclusions — the decree preambles, the introductions and competency rationale, the didactic orientations, the assessment criteria and end-of-cycle expectations, the synthesis tables (verified verbatim repeats of the competency sections), the attitudes/values lists, Book 1's glossary and opening summary table, and the Council of Europe cross-references — are stocked nowhere. Book 3's synthesis table words its C2 end-of-cycle expectation as modelling "situacions cientificotècniques" (scientific-technical) inside the social-sciences programme — a copy-over slip in the decree as published; Book 3's competency section itself is consistently social-sciences, and the slip is copied into no row below. Book 2 states its cone/polyhedron sections twice (forms table and models table, printed pp. 32–33) — counted once in the placements and stocked once. Printed page = PDF page in all four books (Books 2–4 carry the BOPA folio; Book 1's Portal PDF is paginated at the foot the same way). Into this lesson the placements stock 11 teacher-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3, Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Programa de matemàtiques de primera ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 1–6) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 376/2021, Annex VII; ISBN 978-99920-0-934-5; 688,455 bytes, 49 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Programa de matemàtiques de segona ensenyança* (curriculum programme, Grades 7–10) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 355/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); ISBN 978-99920-0-847-2; 1,808,872 bytes, 56 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 3:** *Programa de matemàtiques aplicades a les ciències socials del batxillerat general* (curriculum programme, Grades 11–12) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 374/2023 (BOPA Núm. 91, 19-7-2023); 1,696,001 bytes, 41 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

**Book 4:** *Programa de matemàtiques cientificotècniques del batxillerat general* (curriculum programme, Grades 11–12) — Andorra, Govern d'Andorra, Escola Andorrana; Decret 242/2025 (BOPA Núm. 83, 9-7-2025); ISBN 978-99920-82-23-2; 4,325,201 bytes, 38 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page (the BOPA folio). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — an official curriculum programme: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself.

Andorra enters this lesson as teacher evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count above — every book in Andorra's in-hand set is an official curriculum programme, not a pupil textbook, cited as the evidence kind it is, never counted as a pupil's book (the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent). Andorra's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): all 4 programmes are read and stocked, but no pupil textbook is in hand — none exists to hold for the Escola Andorrana — and the Spanish and French systems' maths manuals are list-only names with no files held.

## Angola added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Angola's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **6 books, all pupil textbooks: Matemática, Manuais da 1.ª–6.ª Classe (Ministério da Educação — MED, Angola; updated editions of 2021, in Portuguese). There is no teacher guide in the Angola Maths set in hand, so every Angola row in this course is a pupil-textbook row — there are no teacher-evidence rows, and Angola counts in every lesson it stocks** — and placed 229 teaching items. The expansion map's “6 student + 0 teacher = 6” is correct: download-lists/angola.md, the phase3-staging checklist, textbooks.tsv, phase3-push-state.json (“pushed”) and the staging books.json files all give the same six distinct pupil textbooks, and nothing else. None of the six PDFs was on local disk (pusher-secured only), so all six were fetched fresh for the read from the books' recorded second official source, the ministry's Xilonga host, byte-exact against the records. One set nuance: the portal's 32-manual set contains no 2.ª Classe Matemática at all — the 2.ª book in hand (Book 2) exists only as an unlinked Xilonga file (MAT02-LR.pdf) found by pattern probe on 2026-10-23. A Guia Prático Matemática 6ª exists only as an exact-link reader copy and is NOT in hand — cited nowhere; if one is ever secured it enters as teacher evidence only. The Book 2 and Book 4 PDFs carry a massively duplicated text layer (the same text overprinted many times per page) — a production flaw of the files; the content is fully legible and both books were read in full from it. Twelve book errors are recorded in the placements; they are carried in the book entries below and taught corrected. The placements' named exclusions — the apresentação (ministerial foreword letter) and anthem/flag/civic front matter, the bibliografia pages, pure colouring, tracing and copying practice with no new concept, the editorial production notes printed inside the books, and exercise sets as such (exercises practise the item under which they sit) — are stocked nowhere. Printed-page bases: Books 1 and 3 — printed page = PDF page − 1; Books 2, 4, 5 and 6 — printed page = PDF page. Into this lesson the placements stock 5 items (all from pupil textbooks) from Book 5 and Book 6.

**Book 5:** *Matemática — Manual da 5.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 5) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-84-8; 4,730,235 bytes, 128 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book errors, taught corrected: printed p. 29's worked example 12 + 37 + 7 + 3 is printed as = 55 with a garbled regrouping (correct total: 59); printed p. 73's equivalence line prints 1/3 = 2/6 = 2/9 (the last term should be 3/9); printed p. 127's median worked example uses an ordered list containing 12, which is not in the exercise's data, and drops one 10 (the median is taught from the data as printed in the exercise: ordered 6, 7, 9, 10, 10, 10, 11, 13, 14 → median 10).

**Book 6:** *Matemática — Manual da 6.ª Classe* (pupil textbook, Grade 6) — Angola, Ministério da Educação (MED), Texto Editores, 2021, 2.ª Edição, ISBN 978-989-8884-85-5; 2,544,284 bytes, 104 PDF pp. Pages cited are the book's printed pages (printed page = PDF page). Book notes, taught corrected: printed p. 51 first presents the ambiguous expression 28 : 7 × 2 with two candidate readings and tells the pupil to ask the teacher — the rule is fixed by the worked examples on printed p. 52, and the course teaches the standard order (× and ÷ in order, before + and −); printed p. 62's direct-proportionality worked line prints the third ratio with denominator 6 where the sequence's term is 5 (150/5 = 30 — taught from the sequence). The book carries printer's proof marks, and one running footer misprints “Matemática 5ª Classe” (printed p. 99) — production matter, cited nowhere.

## Bahamas added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read the Bahamas' whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **4 documents, all of them Bahamas Ministry of Education curriculum or examination-syllabus documents; 0 pupil (student) textbooks and 0 teacher guides** — and placed 496 requirement items. A curriculum states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself. Every Bahamas item in this section is therefore CURRICULUM EVIDENCE — a statement of what the Bahamas requires, worded as a requirement — cited as the evidence kind it is (Book 3's items are examination-SYLLABUS evidence, on the same standing); the Bahamas is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana/Barbados evidence-only precedent, until a Bahamas pupil book is in hand and read. Set correction, as the placements prove it: the expansion map recorded the in-hand set as “4 student + 0 teacher” — the count of 4 is right, the kind is wrong; all four documents are ministry requirement documents (kind = curriculum in both the staging books.json files and textbooks.tsv). The file the records title *BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9* is actually the *Bahamas Junior Certificate 044 Mathematics Syllabus 2003* — a 10-page scanned examination syllabus with no text layer, read visually page by page — and the held Senior High file is the July 2019 revision of the Grades 10–12 guidelines; both are placed as what they are. No pupil textbook or teacher guide is in hand: the pupil books Bahamas schools use are commercial (Hodder *Bahamas Primary Mathematics* Books 1–6 — existence verified in the records, no file held; Books 1–2 name Silver Burdett Ginn Mathematics and Harcourt Math in classroom use, and Book 4's bibliography names STP Caribbean Mathematics, Mathematics: A Complete Course, Fundamental Mathematics for the Caribbean and Nelson Caribbean Mathematics — named only, none held), and the one school-compiled pupil text with an exact link in the records (Akhepran International Academy, *Mathematics BJC Textbook — Grade 7*, 2023) was never downloaded and sits in no record set, so it is not part of this read and is cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered objective in each guidelines book's Scope of Work body — the Content column read with every objective — Book 3 at one item per numbered topic row of its Topics table and Book 4 at one item per CONCEPTS/TOPICS block; an objective restated in full on a continuation page is counted once, at its first full statement, and the two requirements the documents restate inside a second strand (Book 1 Grade 2 estimation, item 69; Book 2 Grade 5 rounding of decimals, item 256) are stocked as the placements place them, each row naming the restatement. The placements' named exclusions — Benchmarks, Scope-and-Sequence charts, pacing guides and Essential Questions in Books 1–2; the Problems of the Day sets, Materials Lists, assessment rubrics, the Children's Literature list and References (record items R1–R7 and R9–R11); Book 3's examination administration, its 19 general aims and its erratum sheet (R8); Book 4's Scope and Sequence charts, Glossary and Technology Integration pages — are stocked nowhere. Book errors found in the read are carried corrected on the rows they touch. Into this lesson the placements stock 11 requirement-evidence items from Book 1, Book 2, Book 3 and Book 4.

**Book 1:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 1–3* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,558,181 bytes, 263 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 51. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 2:** *Primary School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 4–6* (curriculum guidelines) — Bahamas, Ministry of Education, Department of Education, September 2010; 5,278,639 bytes, 328 PDF pages; Scope of Work printed page = PDF page − 54. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — a curriculum states what is required to be taught; it teaches nothing itself.

The library records title this file “BJC Mathematics Curriculum, Grades 7–9” — the held file is the 2003 examination syllabus, and it is placed as what it is (set correction, stated here; the records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records). SYLLABUS EVIDENCE ONLY — an examination syllabus states what candidates are required to know; it teaches nothing itself.

**Book 4:** *Senior High School Mathematics Curriculum Guidelines, Grades 10–12* (curriculum guidelines; the held file is the July 2019 revision, footer “High School Mathematics Curriculum – The Bahamas, 2019”) — Bahamas, Department of Education; 3,766,723 bytes, 219 PDF pages; printed page = PDF page. ## Belize added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Belize's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **3 documents, all of them Belize Ministry of Education *Mathematics Curriculum: Teacher's Guide* books (August 2012) — Infant 1, Standard 1 and Standard IV; 0 pupil (student) textbooks** — and placed 108 teaching items. Every document in the set is a teacher's guide: it states the learning outcomes pupils must reach and teaches the teacher how to teach each one (comments, sample lesson objectives, specific content, sample activities and assessment). Every Belize item in this section is therefore TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE — Belize's guides teach teachers how to teach the topic; no Belize pupil textbook is in hand — cited as the evidence kind it is; Belize is counted in NO lesson's taught-in figure, on the standing Ghana evidence-only precedent, until a Belize pupil book is in hand and read. No pupil Maths book is in hand: the pupil books on the official National Textbook Program list (*My BZ Math* Infant 1–Standard 4, Macmillan; *Caribbean Primary Math* Bk 6 for Standard 5, Hodder; *Active Math* for Standard 6, Macmillan) are all recorded missing in the library files, and the guides' own textbook linkages point to the legacy pupil series *Primary Mathematics for Belize* (Hodder, by term codes Y1T1…/S1T1…), which is named by the guides only — not in hand, not read, and not a course source. Three further Maths documents — the *Primary curriculum: Mathematics* division webs (Lower Division, Middle Division, Upper Division) — are recorded at exact link only, never downloaded and in no books.json; they are not part of this read and are cited nowhere. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct numbered learning outcome in each guide's Standards and Learning Outcomes section (35 outcomes in each guide, 105 in all) plus 3 items taught in a detailed section but stated in no outcome of that guide (items 64, 65 and 87, marked D); outcome numbering is continuous across the series (Infant 1 carries M1.1–M20.1, Standard 1 continues at M1.10, Standard IV at M1.16), which is why the codes do not restart at 1 in the later guides. Printed page = PDF page in all three guides. The placements' named exclusions — the guides' front matter (programme philosophy: goals, rationale, assessment discussion), strand introductions and Big Ideas prose, sample lesson objectives, sample assessments, sample activities and print/technology resource columns as apparatus, the shared manipulatives list, the websites lists, the shared Mathematics Glossary (carried in the placements as record item R2 — a teacher reference list, cited nowhere as content), and the standards each guide marks N/A at its level (Book 1: M2 place value and M13 equations; Book 2: M14 measurement concepts; Book 3: M5 position/ranking numbers and M14) — are stocked nowhere. The 14 book errors and inconsistencies found in the read (placements E1–E14) are carried corrected on the rows they touch: item 35 is placed as data interpretation, not probability, though the guide files it under its probability standard (E5); item 51's set language is taught with “complement” for the book's “compliment” (E6); item 66's many-to-one correspondence is taught as one symbol or unit standing for many objects, not the guide's function-style definition (E8); item 82 is stocked WITHOUT the outcome's fourth bullet (“expand powers with same base to add or subtract”), which is not a valid add/subtract procedure (E9); item 81 is placed as stated and flagged as vague and advanced for its level (E10); item 104 is placed as measurement and taught with the standard measurement meanings, the guide's own definition of precision being a data-management sense (E13); and item 106 follows the guide's own correct definitions — its assessment text's “medium” for median is an error (E12). Into this lesson the placements stock 2 teacher-guide-evidence items from Book 2 and Book 3.

Standards and Learning Outcomes section printed pp. 12–16; detailed teaching sections printed pp. 17–56. Printed page = PDF page. Outcome numbering continues the series from Book 1 (M1.10 onwards). TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide teaches the teacher how to teach the topic; it is not a pupil textbook.

## Australia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Australia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **9 books, all of them student textbooks** (Years 8–10: eight Cambridge volumes — the Essential Mathematics Australian Curriculum, Victorian Curriculum and CambridgeMATHS NSW lines, plus the GOLD support edition and the CORE pathway volume — and one Jacaranda volume, Maths Quest 10+10A) — and placed 844 teaching items. Australia has no government textbook series (ACARA writes the Australian Curriculum but publishes and prescribes no textbooks); the set is the school-used commercial series the country records name, and every book is cited as such. All 9 files are distinct works (distinct ISBNs, extents and contents, verified per book on the read); the publisher-line relations are stated in the placements: Book A1 (GOLD) is the support edition of Book A2's Year 8 material, Book A3 is the same line in its Victorian ©2024 edition, Books A4 and A6 are the NSW syllabus editions, and Book A9 is Book A8's CORE pathway companion. In the sections below the books are cited as Books A1–A9. Australia COUNTS in this lesson's taught-in figure: every item is pupil-textbook teaching. The read's four flagged claims are carried exactly: (F1) Book A3's contents lists its Chapter 11, Algorithmic thinking, as “(online only)”, but the file in hand contains the full chapter, bound after the Answers (printed pp. 926–941) — it is read and placed, and it founds this course's algorithmic-thinking lesson; (F2) Book A7's contents lists a Chapter 18, Algorithmic thinking, with no page numbers, and the chapter is NOT in the file (searched end to end; the book closes with its Glossary, printed p. 1106, and Index, printed p. 1117) — recorded as absent, not read, and no item rests on it; (F3) Book A9's running head at printed p. 644 prints section 10E's code as “0E” — the book's own production error; the placement cites 10E, as the section's heading and contents give it; (F4) only the 9 Maths books are this read's set — the country records' wider Australia picture (12 staged books across subjects, plus filled-by-exact-link titles) is not merged into this count, and in particular the two senior Maths titles that exist only as exact-link records (Cambridge Senior Specialist Mathematics, QCE Units 3&4, 2e, and Cambridge Senior Mathematical Methods, QCE Units 3&4, 2e) are NOT in hand and were NOT read. The placements' named exclusions — front matter and “how to use this book” guides; every chapter's pre-tests, progress quizzes, unlettered applications / problem-solving / modelling / investigation spreads, puzzles and games, chapter summaries, checklists and reviews, semester reviews, glossaries, indexes and answer sections — are apparatus/assessment, teach no new content, and are stocked nowhere. The set is Years 8–10 secondary only, so the primary lessons (counting, place value, first steps) receive no Australia item — that is the set's shape, not a reading gap. Australia's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand book (9/9) has been read and placed; the two senior QCE exact-link titles do not block the in-hand class (if exact-link titles are ever ruled in-scope for the courses reckoning, Australia drops to PARTIAL until they are grabbed and read). Into this lesson the placements stock 15 items from Book A1, Book A2, Book A3, Book A4, Book A5, Book A6, Book A7, Book A8, Book A9.

Jorge Díaz Porras) and the ITCR Proyecto EVEPRIM primary books for Grades 3 and 6 (10 textos + 10 folletos de prácticas across Relaciones y Álgebra, Números, Geometría, Medidas, Estadística and Probabilidad); 0 teacher guides for Costa Rica Maths exist in hand** — and placed 229 teaching items. **SET CORRECTION: the expansion map's count of 17 student is WRONG** — 17 is exactly the number of Costa Rica Mathematics rows in textbooks.tsv (10 EVEPRIM textos + only 2 of the 10 EVEPRIM folletos + 5 CONED antologías); the TSV was never updated for 8 folletos staged on 2026-10-25, and the staging books.json files and phase3-push-state.json agree on the true set of 25 (the folletos count student-side, per the map's own stated basis). All 25 were grabbed, verified (byte size + MD5 against books.json; the two Relaciones y Álgebra textos, which have no books.json entry, verified by size + title page as the main-repo files) and read in full — no partial excerpts, no duplicates (the SharePoint folder copies of the six texto books matched the staged sizes byte-for-byte, so they are the same files, cited once) and no unopenable files. The three Grade 3 textos were read in their November 2026 editions, the in-hand editions per books.json; the GitHub release mid-size-books-3 still holds the superseded October editions of those three textos (a record-level flag, reported, not fixed — same books, older exports; only the November editions are cited here). Two chapter promises in the CONED books are NOT teaching and place no item anywhere: CONED 7's Capítulo 4 is titled Estadística y Probabilidad but teaches statistics only (probability appears only in its introductory discussion questions, pp. 209–214), and CONED 10's Capítulo 1 objectives promise plane transformations that no Tema in the chapter teaches (only a recall of Grade 8 homothety). The placements' named exclusions — front matter, indices, bibliographies and answer keys (soluciones); the EVEPRIM answer-key chapters and the review chapters that re-teach already-placed sections; the CONED chapter motto/introduction pages and “desafío matemático” pages; and the videos and web interactives the EVEPRIM books link to — are apparatus and are stocked nowhere. The 5 CONED book-error groups found in the read — CONED 7: the angle table (p. 106) labels a 360° angle “CONVEXO”, the inverse-proportionality example (pp. 201–203) states v·t = 247.5 for a product of 330 and “very close to 3 hours” at 78 km/h for ≈4.23 h with garbled fraction work, the triangle inequality (p. 125) omits “any two” sides, the median answer (p. 238) gives 68 for a median of 67, the worked powers page (p. 64) misaligns its results, and a tiling exercise (p. 35) reads “largo por largo”; CONED 8: the table of contents numbers the equations Tema “Tema 4” while the body heads it “Tema 5” (numbering slip only); CONED 9: the SI prefix table (p. 74) gives exa the symbol “R” (exa is E), the radian-to-degree rule (p. 117) is printed inverted/garbled though its worked examples are correct, and the contents duplicates “Función Cuadrática” for Capítulo 3 Temas 1 and 4 and mislabels Capítulo 4 as “Capítulo III”; CONED 10: the falling-body model (p. 373) prints “12” for ½ (the worked values use ½ correctly), the key (p. 42) duplicates “tangentes” where “secantes” is meant, p. 14 asks for the area of a “circunferencia” (the circle), and the pentagon solution (p. 86) drops its degree symbols; CONED 11: the logarithm table (p. 118) misaligns its examples (it prints log₅ 5 = 2, which belongs to log₅ 25), the wound-model solution (p. 172) shows an unsigned division display before the correct result, and “invectiva” (p. 121) is a typo for inyectiva — are carried corrected on the rows they touch. One further note is carried as the placements carry it: the Medidas G3 answer key's building-height estimate reads 45 dam in the October copy and “4 dam” in the November extraction — treated as a re-export/extraction artefact in the key, not teaching content, and cited nowhere. Costa Rica's books are pupil books, so Costa Rica is COUNTED: it joins this lesson's acceptance count wherever the placements stock an item. CONED printed page = PDF page throughout; EVEPRIM pages are cited by PDF page with the book's section numbers (two exceptions print folios: the EVEPRIM 6 Probabilidad texto's printed folio = PDF page − 6 and the EVEPRIM 3 Medidas texto's = PDF page − 7; citations give PDF pages). ## Bulgaria added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Bulgaria's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **5 files = 4 works, all pupil textbooks: the Бит и Техника (Bit i Tekhnika) *Математика* for Grades 1, 2 and 3 and for Grade 4 in two part-files (1 част / 2 част), 642 PDF pages in all** — and placed 123 teaching items. **The expansion map's “5 student + 0 teacher” is CORRECT as a file count (5 files, 4 works). Full-book vs sample verdict: NO in-hand Maths file is a sample** — every file's extent matches its own imprint's print-sheet count (Grade 1: 20.5 sheets, 128 lessons to printed p. 158 + 12 appendices; Grade 2: 18 sheets, 112 lessons to printed p. 140; Grade 3: 18 sheets, 112 lessons to printed p. 140; Grade 4: 10 + 11.5 sheets, lessons 1–136), and the 8 proven publisher samples in Bulgaria's library record (Music G6, Foreign Language G6/G7, БЕЛ G10/G11 and Geography G5) contain no Maths book, so the library's sample ruling does not touch this set. All five PDFs are image-only with no usable text layer and were read visually from page renders (cover, imprint, the book's own contents, and body pages for every distinct topic); printed folio = PDF page − 1 in every book, and Grade 4 Part 2 restarts its own pagination (its citations are marked “Part 2”). No Bulgaria Maths book was in the course before this merge — the string “Bulgaria” occurred nowhere under `maths/` — so every placed item is new here. Set boundary: everything else Bulgarian Maths in the records is exact-link / reader only and NOT in hand — Riva Maths G1–G4 PDFs (duplicate slots for the same grades), Klett epub Maths G5/G6 readers, Klett FlipHTML5 Maths G7/G8 (fullness unproven) and Архимед Maths G10/G11 Ourboox PDFs — none is part of this read and none is cited here; if any is later secured and read, Bulgaria reverts to PARTIAL until it is stocked. The placements' named exclusions — each grade's diagnostics, self-checks and consolidation lessons, drills, blitz tournaments, games and projects (other than the Grade 4 data project stocked in the statistics lessons), end-of-unit and year reviews, and the Grade 1 book's 12 cut-out appendices (number cards, shape cut-outs, play money, tangram, clock face, addition wheel, 0–20 ruler) — are apparatus and are stocked nowhere; the topics they practise are counted at the lessons that teach them. **Book-internal flag, carried exactly: the Grade 1 book's introduction (printed p. 4) promises the unit litre, but no lesson teaches it — the contents list no litre lesson and the closing units summary (lesson 127, printed p. 155, read on the body page) lists only hour, euro, eurocent, kilogram and centimetre — so the litre is stocked nowhere, and Bulgaria Grade 1 stands as teaching cm, kg, euro/eurocent and the hour, not the litre.** Book errors proper (wrong mathematics) were found nowhere in the pages read; contents-page slips (e.g. Grade 4 Part 1 listing its angle lessons out of order in the contents) are apparatus slips, not teaching errors, and are counted nowhere. The placements' one NEW LESSON NEEDED topic — calculator use (Grade 4 Part 2, lessons 118–119, item 121) — is stocked in the existing `money-and-time/calculator-use` lesson built by the Sri Lanka expansion merge; no second calculator lesson is built. Into this lesson the placements stock 1 items (item 123).

**Book 4:** *Математика за 4 клас, 1 част* (pupil textbook, Grade 4, part 1) — Bulgaria, Бит и Техника (Bit i Tekhnika), 1st edition 2019, ISBN 978-619-7523-27-0; seven authors headed by Димитрина Капитанова; 17,396,072 bytes, 82 PDF pp.; printed folio = PDF page − 1. FULL BOOK — part 1 of 2 (lessons 1–62, contents to printed p. 79). Pupil textbook.

Every book was grabbed fresh by the authenticated GitHub API route, verified byte-exact against its staging `books.json` byte count and cover/title-proved, and the grabbed copy deleted after reading; the recorded Google Drive fallback is login-walled from this host and delivered no book. The placements' counting grain is one item per distinct taught topic — a named section or a coherent teaching block inside a unit. The placements' named exclusions — the apparatus of all 14 books (unit-opening, group-activity, evaluation, self-assessment, bibliography and book-care pages, except the five group activities that teach a topic found nowhere else in their book, which are placed); the *Primero Volumen I* Matemática diagnostic evaluation; the Primero competency pages; the Primero volumes' non-maths sequences (with two maths-adjacent blocks inside them named in the placements and stocked nowhere); the 5 Nivel Inicial integrated books (a scope note, not part of the Maths set); and one record artefact that is not a book (a stray partial-upload `.part` file in the grade-04 push state) — are stocked nowhere. One book error is carried corrected on the row it touches: *Matemática 3° Primaria*'s weight table (evaluation, printed p. 152; the same table family at p. 147) pairs 8 lb with 48 oz — wrong: 1 lb = 16 oz, so 8 lb = 128 oz (48 oz is 3 lb). Minor slips, no action: the contents lists of *Matemática* 2° and 3° Secundaria run one page behind the printed folios — every citation uses the folios. Checked and NOT an error: *Matemática 4° Secundaria*, printed p. 84, prints Pythagoras as a² = b² + c² with a defined as the hypotenuse — correct as printed. In the sections below the books are cited as Books 1–14 in the placements' order. The Dominican Republic COUNTS in a lesson's taught-in figure on the strength of these student-book rows. The Dominican Republic's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every in-hand Maths book (the 11 dedicated *Matemática* books plus the Primero volumes carrying Grade 1's maths) has been read in full and placed.

Extent, honestly: five whole-course books (TT1, TT2 GL, TT3, English TT4, TT6); three strand volumes complete as published but covering one strand only (TT4 Irish = Strand 2; TT5 Irish = Strand 1; TT7 Irish = Strand 5); and one supplement booklet (the TT5 Forlíonadh — deferred Strand 1 material and the Central Limit Theorem), complete as published, not a standalone textbook. Two record flags ride with the set (the read's flagged claims F1–F2, both about the library records, not the course): **F1** — 8 of the 9 PDFs are absent from their phase3-staging folders, which hold only books.json + CURRICULUM.md; all eight are secured in GitHub and were re-grabbed byte-exact from COGG for the read, and restoring them into staging is owed on the library side. **F2** — the held TT3 is the updated edition (2024) of the 2013 book, with an added Chapter 19, Inferential Statistics, that no record mentions; its three items are stocked from that chapter and TT3's book entry names the edition wherever it appears. The placements' named exclusions — every book's chapter tests (“Cuir triail ort féin”), tasks, learning-experience and review sections, chapter summaries and Answers sections, and every book's prefaces, keyword lists and contents pages — are stocked nowhere. ## Fiji added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Fiji's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **13 pupil textbooks of the Ministry of Education / Curriculum Development Unit series, one for each of Years 1–13** — and placed 1,012 items. The expansion map's count is exact: staging `books.json` holds exactly 13 Mathematics entries (one per year, all `kind: student`) and `phase3-push-state.json` lists all 13 as pushed; no Maths teacher guide is in hand (the AQEP Maths Guides for Lower Primary exist only as AnyFlip reader copies in the download list — not in hand, not counted). The staged PDFs had been pushed and deleted locally, so each book was re-fetched for the read — from the repo tree (Contents API) where the pusher had stored it as a blob, else from the book's recorded `source_url` (a Wayback Machine capture of the ministry file, Beacon Media, or Google Drive) — and the grabbed copies were deleted after their placements were written. The read's nine flagged claims ride with the set, honestly: (1) `textbooks.tsv` carries only 9 of the 13 rows (Years 1, 2, 3, 4, 7, 10, 11, 12, 13) — Years 5, 6, 8 and 9 are staged and pushed but missing from the TSV; (2) the Year 2 scan duplicates printed p. 57 and printed p. 58 is absent, so the Money unit's Activity 12 is not in hand to read and is cited nowhere; (3) the Year 5 capture has no cover/title leaves — the file opens at the Strand 1 divider and the body runs complete, Strand 1 p. 1 to Strand 5 p. 98, its identity resting on the staged record and the body's own structure; (4) Year 4's folio sequence is irregular — folios 44–48 and 54 never appear and folios 42 and 55 each appear twice — but the leaf count closes exactly (85 printed numbers − 6 absent + 2 duplicated = 81 body leaves), every contents strand and unit is present and no activity breaks mid-stream at the gaps, so the evidence points to the printed book's own folio misprints rather than missing leaves, a judgement the file alone cannot prove; (5) Year 3's scan order is scrambled at the end (contents folio ii and printed pp. 46, 51, 52 sit at PDF pp. 76–80) and one activity page appears twice (folioed 47 and 51 — counted once), with folio tracking accounting for all of printed pp. 1–75; (6) Year 10 exists in two packagings — the staged Beacon Media file (9,099,595 bytes, 202 pp.) is the copy cited; an ECopies capture of the same book is 9,079,205 bytes / 200 pp.; (7) Year 12's first Wayback transfer arrived truncated (11,794,148 bytes, broken cross-reference table) and was discarded — a fresh download delivered the complete book (17,085,310 bytes, 284 pp.), which is what was read; (8) Year 1's contents lists Chance at p. 65 and Data at p. 66 where the folios are 64 and 65 — the body count closes exactly, so nothing is missing and the contents is one out; (9) Year 6's interior copyright and acknowledgement pages misname it the “Year 7” book — a copy-paste error in the book itself; its cover metadata, contents, running heads and body are Year 6 throughout, and it is read and placed as Year 6. Book errors found in the read are taught corrected, the corrected form being what the items teach — among them Year 3's conversion-table lines “1,000 cm = 1 km” (correct: 100,000 cm) and “10,000 mm = 1,000 m” (correct: 10 m), Year 4's banner “30 minutes = 1 hour” against its own body's 60, Year 5's “1 000 millimetres = 1 litre”, Year 9's contents “Common Multitudes” (for Common Multiples) and its (a × b)ᵐ = aᵐ × bⁿ misprint, and Year 1's count-in-twos strip printing 9 where 19 belongs; Year 10 carries its own printed errata list (pp. 201–202), whose corrections are noted in the items. Counting grain: one item per distinct teaching point — a named concept, method, rule or worked skill with its printed page. The placements' named exclusions — strand divider pages; contents and acknowledgement pages; pure exercise sets and worked-example repeats of an item already placed; Year 1's “Checking my number” ten-frame reference page (printed p. 32); Year 3's duplicated capacity page (counted once); Year 4's closing multiplication-table chart (printed p. 86); Year 10's printed errata list (pp. 201–202, apparatus — its corrections ride in the items); Year 12's closing motivational quotes and exam tips (printed pp. 273–275); Year 13's bibliography and back matter — are stocked nowhere. ## Honduras added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Honduras's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **21 files = 18 distinct works: 9 pupil textbooks (Grades 3–9, Grade 10 Matemática I and Grade 11 Matemática III BCH) and 12 teacher-guide files for Grades 1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10 and 11, the Grade 8 guide held in two editions (2018 and the 2022 revision) and the Grade 1 and 2 guides each in two scans** — all from the Secretaría de Educación's PROMETAM / UPNFM series produced with JICA support — and placed 335 items (218 pupil-textbook items and 117 teacher-evidence items), plus 3 duplicate-edition records (placements items 241, 242 and 338) that are cited once with their works and carry no separate items. The expansion map's “9 student + 12 teacher” count is right: the staging `books.json` files, the `phase3-push-state.json` pushed set and `textbooks.tsv` agree on exactly these 21 files. There is no Grade 1–2 pupil textbook in the Secretaría's programme (workbook only — SE Manual de Lineamientos), so the Grade 1–2 teaching in hand is the two guides; there is no Grade 3 or Grade 6 teacher guide in hand. Every file was grabbed from its recorded `source_url` (educatrachos.hn / libroshonduras.com) after the staged PDFs had been pushed and deleted locally, and verified by exact byte size against the download-list record and by page count against the `books.json` record before reading; grabbed copies were deleted after each book's text was extracted. Read basis, stated honestly: the six text-layer pupil books and eight text-layer guides were read in full from their text; the three scanned pupil books (Grades 7–9) were read by OCR of their Índices and unit/lesson openers with interior pages OCR-sampled — Grades 7 and 8 cross-checked against the full text-layer read of their own teacher guides, which plan every lesson class by class — and the two 2022 guides are themselves scans, OCR-read at cover, presentation and index level, the Grade 9 guide therefore placed at unit grain. Counting grain: one item per Índice lesson; a lesson teaching two distinct course topics is split into two items, and three items (a Grade 4 pupil lesson, a Grade 10 pupil lesson and a Grade 2 guide unit) each carry a row in two lessons below and are counted once. The placements' named exclusions — the “Páginas para recortar” cut-out pages of the Grades 3, 4 and 5 pupil books (manipulatives, not teaching text), the Grade 6 book's closing “Repaso” review, and the “Nos divertimos” / “¿Sabías que?” enrichment boxes inside placed lessons — are stocked nowhere. Printed-page basis: PESD I modules — printed page = PDF page − 1 (footer-verified); PESD II modules — printed page = the contents-page foliation; the *Manual do Professor* — printed page = PDF page. Counting grain: one item per lesson of a module where the lesson teaches one course topic; a lesson is split into two items where it teaches two clearly distinct course topics. Book errors found on the read are taught corrected, as the placements record them: PESD II Módulo 3’s cover subtitle wrongly repeats Módulo 1’s (“Conjuntos Numéricos e Cálculo Algébrico”) — its interior is exponential and logarithmic functions throughout — and its answer section is headed “Soluções Modulo 1” (sic) although it is Módulo 3’s own key; PESD II Módulo 9’s answer section is headed “Soluções Módulo 8” (sic) although it is Módulo 9’s own key; PESD II Módulo 5’s contents numbers two different lessons “Lição 4” (the linear-function revision, printed p. 43, and the modulus function, printed p. 55) — both lessons are placed, as items 177 and 178 of this read; and PESD I Módulo 4’s contents page omits its Lição 13, which exists in the body at printed p. 64 and is placed (item 96), while its Lições 14 and 15 carry identical stated objectives and are placed as two items as printed (items 97 and 98). The placements’ record items are apparatus, stocked nowhere: R1 — every PESD module’s closing preparation test (teste de preparação), answer key (chave de correcção / soluções) and bibliography; R2 — the *Manual do Professor*’s answer key to the 1.ª Classe pupil book (printed pp. 174–183; the pupil book itself is not in hand); R3 — the DGD Módulo 1 spot-check copy, fetched only to verify its subject identity. The placements’ named exclusions are stocked nowhere: DGD (Desenho e Geometria Descritiva, PESD II Módulos 1, 3–12 — a separate named subject of the Mozambican curriculum with its own module series, interior-checked on Módulo 1 and excluded as such; Módulo 2 is not in hand at all); all other PESD subjects in hand (Biologia, Química, Física, Geografia, História, Inglês, Português, Francês, TIC, Agro-pecuária, Empreendedorismo, Educação Visual, Filosofia) — separate curriculum subjects, none a Matemática book in disguise; the *Manual do Professor*’s Língua Portuguesa and Educação Física parts (printed pp. 4–145 and 184–215); and the primary print-programme Matemática books (1ª–6ª, Edição revista 2025), which are link-only, not in hand and not read. Pupil items are COUNTED normally; the teacher-evidence items (T1–T6, from the one teacher guide) are named in the rows and stay outside the acceptance count. Into this lesson the placements stock 1 item — 1 pupil-textbook row — from Book 5 (*Matemática — Módulo 5 (PESD I)*).
